javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n\n/**\n * Password-based or key-based authenticated symmetric encryption for application\n * secrets, built only on the JDK's own crypto APIs (javax.crypto + java.security).\n *\n * Cipher:  AES-256 in GCM mode (authenticated encryption, 128-bit tag).\n * Keys:    either a raw 256-bit AES key, or a passphrase stretched with\n *          PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 (310,000 iterations by default).\n * Output:  a single portable text token, \"aesgcm$v1&base64url(header||ciphertext||tag)\".\n *\n * Each call to encrypt() draws a fresh 16-byte salt and a fresh 12-byte IV from\n * SecureRandom, so encrypting the same value twice never yields the same token.\n * The header (version, KDF id, iteration count, salt, IV) is fed to GCM as\n * additional authenticated data, so flipping any header byte makes decryption\n * fail instead of silently changing how the key is derived.\n *\n * See README.md for the token layout, the CLI, and the security notes.\n */\npublic final class SymmetricCrypto {\n\n    /** Text prefix written in front of every token, so a token is self-describing. */\n    public static final String SCHEME_PREFIX = \"aesgcm$v1$\";\n\n    private static final byte VERSION = 1;\n    private static final byte KDF_PBKDF2_SHA256 = 1;\n    private static final byte KDF_RAW_KEY = 2;\n\n    private static final int SALT_LEN = 16;\n    private static final int IV_LEN = 12;\n    private static final int TAG_BITS = 128;\n    private static final int KEY_BITS = 256;\n    private static final int KEY_BYTES = KEY_BITS / 8;\n\n    /** OWASP 2023 floor for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 310_000;\n    private static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 100_000;\n    private static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 5_000_000;\n\n    /** Guard against unbounded memory use on hostile or accidental input. */\n    public static final int MAX_INPUT_BYTES = 64 * 1024 * 1024;\n\n    private static final SecureRandom RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n    private static final Base64.Encoder TOKEN_ENCODER =\n            Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding();\n    private static final Base64.Decoder TOKEN_DECODER = Base64.getUrlDecoder();\n\n    /** Strategy for turning a token's header back into the key that sealed it. */\n    public interface KeyResolver {\n        SecretKey resolve(byte kdfId, int iterations, byte[] salt) throws GeneralSecurityException;\n    }\n\n    private SymmetricCrypto() {\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- keying\n\n    /** Generates a fresh random 256-bit AES key, Base64 encoded for storage. */\n    public static String generateBase64Key() {\n        byte[] key = new byte[KEY_BYTES];\n        RANDOM.nextBytes(key);\n        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(key);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes a Base64 key (standard or URL-safe alphabet) into an AES key.\n     *\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the text is not Base64 or not 16/24/32 bytes\n     */\n    public static SecretKey aesKeyFromBase64(String base64) {\n        byte[] raw;\n        try {\n            raw = Base64.getMimeDecoder().decode(base64.trim());\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"key is not valid Base64\", e);\n        }\n        try {\n            return new SecretKeySpec(raw, \"AES\");\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Stretches a passphrase into an AES key. The caller keeps ownership of\n     * {@code passphrase}; it is not modified here (PBEKeySpec copies it).\n     */\n    public static SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt, int iterations)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        if (passphrase == null || passphrase.length == 0) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"passphrase is empty\");\n        }\n        if (salt == null || salt.length < 8) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"salt is too short\");\n        }\n        if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\n                    \"iteration count \" + iterations + \" is outside the accepted range \"\n                            + MIN_ITERATIONS + \"..\" + MAX_ITERATIONS);\n        }\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(passphrase, salt, iterations, KEY_BITS);\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(\"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\");\n            byte[] encoded = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n            try {\n                return new SecretKeySpec(encoded, \"AES\");\n            } finally {\n                Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            }\n        } finally {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void requireAESKey(SecretKey key) {\n        if (key == null || !\"AES\".equalsIgnoreCase(key.getAlgorithm())) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"an AES key is required\");\n        }\n        byte[] encoded = key.getEncoded();\n        if (encoded == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"key material is not extractable\");\n        }\n        Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------ encrypt\n\n    /** Encrypts with a passphrase-derived key using {@link #DEFAULT_ITERATIONS}. */\n    public static String encryptWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, byte[] plaintext)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        return encryptWithPassphrase(passphrase, plaintext, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    }\n\n    /** Encrypts with a passphrase-derived key, stretching it with PBKDF2. */\n    public static String encryptWithPassphrase(char[] passphrase, byte[] plaintext, int iterations)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        requirePlaintext(plaintext);\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_LEN];\n        RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n        SecretKey key = deriveKey(passphrase, salt, iterations);\n        try {\n            return seal(key, plaintext, KDF_PBKDF2_SHA256, iterations, salt);\n        } finally {\n            zero(key);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Encrypts with a caller-supplied AES key (no KDF, no salt). */\n    public static String encryptWithKey(SecretKey key, byte[] plaintext)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        requirePlaintext(plaintext);\n        requireAESKey(key);\n        return seal(key, plaintext, KDF_RAW_KEY, 0, new byte[0]);\n    }\n\n    private static void requirePlaintext(byte[] plaintext) IllegalArgumentException(\"input of \" + plaintext.length\n                    + \" bytes exceeds the \" + MAX_INPUT_BYTES + \" byte limit\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Core seal. Header is authenticated as additional authenticated data, so the\n     * returned ciphertext is bound to the exact salt/IV/iteration parameters used.\n     */\n    private static String seal(SecretKey key, byte[] plaintext, byte kdfId, int iterations,\n                               byte[] salt) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        byte[] iv = new byte[IV_LEN];\n        RANDOM.nextBytes(iv);\n\n        ByteBuffer header = ByteBuffer.allocate(2 + Integer.BYTES + 1 + salt.length\n                + 1 + iv.length);\n        header.put(VERSION)\n                .put(kdfId)\n                .putInt(iterations)\n                .put((byte) salt.length).put(salt)\n                .put((byte) iv.length).put(iv);\n        byte[] headerBytes = header.array();\n\n        Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(\"AES/GCM/NoPadding\");\n        cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, key, new GCMParameterSpec(TAG_BITS, iv));\n        cipher.updateAAD(headerBytes);\n        byte[] body = cipher.doFinal(plaintext);\n\n        ByteBuffer token = ByteBuffer.allocate(headerBytes.length + body.length);\n        token.put(headerBytes).put(body);\n        Arrays.fill(iv, (byte) 0);\n        return SCHEME_PREFIX + TOKEN_ENCODER.encodeToString(token.array());\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------ decrypt\n\n    /** Decrypts a token with a passphrase. */\n    public static byte[] decryptWithPassphrase(String token, char[] passphrase)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        return decrypt(token, (kdfId, iterations, salt) -> {\n            if (kdfId != KDF_PBKDF2_SHA256) {\n                throw new GeneralSecurityException(\n                        \"token was sealed with a raw key, not a passphrase; use --key\");\n            }\n            return deriveKey(passphrase, salt, iterations);\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Decrypts a token with a raw AES key. */\n    public static byte[] decryptWithKey(String token, SecretKey key)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        return decrypt(token, (kdfId, iterations, salt) -> {\n            if (kdfId != KDF_RAW_KEY) {\n        return key;\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Decrypts a token, obtaining the key through {@code resolver}. */\n    public static byte[] decrypt(String token, KeyResolver resolver)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        if (token == null) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"token is null\");\n        }\n        String trimmed = token.trim();\n        if (!trimmed.startsWith(SCHEME_PREFIX)) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\n                    \"token does not start with the expected prefix \" + SCHEME_PREFIX);\n        }\n + iv.length);\n        }\n        if (kdfId == KDF_RAW_KEY && (iterations != 0 || salt.length != 0)) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"raw-key token must carry no salt or iterations\");\n        }\n        if (kdfId == KDF_PBKDF2_SHA256 && salt.length < 8) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"token carries a salt that is too short\");\n        }\n\n tag\");\n        }\n        byte[] headerBytes = Arrays.copyOf(payload, in.position());\n\n        SecretKey key = resolver.resolve(kdfId, iterations, salt);\n        Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(\"AES/GCM/NoPadding\");\n        cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, key, new GCMParameterSpec(TAG_BITS, iv));\n        cipher.updateAAD(headerBytes);\n        try {\n            return cipher.doFinal(body);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(iv, (byte) 0);\n            Arrays.fill(salt, (byte) 0);\n            zero(key);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] readVarField(ByteBuffer in, String name, int maxLen)\n            throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        if (!in.hasRemaining()) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"token is truncated: missing \" + name);\n        }\n        int len = in.get() & 0xFF;\n        if (len > maxLen || len > in.remaining()) {\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"token declares an invalid \" + name\n                    + \" length (\" + len + \")\");\n        }\n        byte[] out = new byte[len];\n        in.get(out);\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------- self-test\n\n    /**\n     * Round-trip and tamper-detection checks. c.fail(\"unexpected exception escaped the self test: \" + e);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(pw, '\\0');\n            Arrays.fill(wrong, '\\0');\n        }\n        return c.failures;\n    }\n\n    /** Re-encodes a token with one payload byte XORed. Test-only. */\n    private static String mutate(String token, int tokenCharIndex, byte xor) {\n        byte[] payload = TOKEN_DECODER.decode(token.substring(SCHEME_PREFIX.length()));\n        int i = tokenCharIndex - SCHEME_PREFIX.length();\n        if (i < 0 || i >= payload.length) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"mutation index out of range: \" + i);\n        }\n        payload[i] ^= xor;\n        return SCHEME_PREFIX + TOKEN_ENCODER.encodeToString(payload);\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isBase64Url(String s) {\n        for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {\n            char ch = s.charAt(i);\n            boolean ok = (ch >= 'A' && ch <= 'Z') || (ch >= 'a' && ch <= 'z')\n                    || (ch >= '0' && ch <= '9') || ch == '-' || ch == '_';\n            if (!ok) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private static final class Checks {\n        private final PrintStream out;\n        private int checks;\n        private int failures;\n\n        Checks(PrintStream out) {\n            this.out = out;\n        }\n\n        void check(String label, boolean ok) {\n            checks++;\n            if (ok) {\n                out.println(\"  PASS  \" + label);\n            } else {\n                failures++;\n                out.println(\"  FAIL  \" + label);\n            }\n        }\n\n        void expectFailure(String label, Class<? extends Throwable> expected,\n                           ThrowingCall call) {\n            checks++;\n            try {\n                call.call();\n                failures++;\n                out.println(\"  FAIL  \" + label + \" (nothing was thrown)\");\n            } catch (Throwable e) {\n                if (expected.isInstance(e)) {\n                    out.println(\"  PASS  \" + label);\n                } else {\n                    failures++;\n                    out.println(\"  FAIL  \" + label + \" (threw \" + e.getClass().getName() + \")\");\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        void fail(String label) {\n            checks++;\n            failures++;\n            out.println(\"  FAIL  \" + label);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private interface ThrowingCall {\n        Object call() throws Exception;\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------ CLI plumbing\n\n    public static void out.println(generateBase64Key());\n                return 0;\n            }\n            case \"demo\": {\n                demo(out);\n                return 0;\n            }\n            case No\nthird-party libraries, no build tool.\n\nIt is an *authenticated* cipher: a wrong key or a modified token produces a hard\nerror rather than garbage plaintext.\n\n```\n$ java SymmetricCrypto encrypt --key \"$(cat key.b64)\" 'db-password=s3cr3t'\naesgcm$v1$AQIAAAAAAAzolQfLMQvXbr2ImaS_hFDhQ1tgL18c4Ql9OyyezVj7dEIiiQuSpPOETQWyP4qA\n\n$ java SymmetricCrypto decrypt --key \"$(cat key.b64)\" 'aesgcm$v1$AQIAAAAAAAz...P4qA'\ndb-password=s3cr3t\n```\n\n## Requirements\n\n- JDK 17 or newer (`java` and `javac` on `PATH`). Uses nothing outside the JDK.\n- Verified on OpenJDK 17.0.20.1.\n\n## Compile and run\n\n```bash\njavac SymmetricCrypto.java                     # produces SymmetricCrypto.class\njava SymmetricCrypto selftest                  # 28 built-in checks, exits 0 when healthy\njava SymmetricCrypto demo                      # one encrypt/decrypt round trip, both key modes\njava SymmetricCrypto keygen > key.b64          # new random 256-bit key, Base64\njava SymmetricCrypto encrypt --key \"$(cat key.b64)\" 'my secret'\njava SymmetricCrypto decrypt --key \"$(cat key.b64)\" 'aesgcm$v1$...'\n```\n\nTo keep the tree tidy, compile into a separate directory:\n\n```bash\njavac -d classes SymmetricCrypto.java\njava -cp classes SymmetricCrypto selftest\n```\n\n## What it does\n\n### Cryptographic design\n\n| Choice | Value | Why |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Cipher | `AES/GCM/NoPadding`, 128-bit tag | Authenticated encryption. GCM catches both wrong keys and tampering; an unauthenticated mode such as CBC would happily return garbage. |\n| Key, mode A | Passphrase -> `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256`, 310,000 rounds, 256-bit key | Turns a human passphrase into a full-strength key. The round count follows the OWASP PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 guidance. (The JDK ships no Argon2/scrypt, so PBKDF2 is the strongest standard option available here.) |\n| Key, mode B | Raw 128/192/256-bit AES key | For machine-generated keys stored in a secret manager - no KDF cost, no passphrase policy to enforce. |\n| Salt | 16 fresh random bytes per message | Same passphrase + same text never yields the same token; kills precomputed/rainbow attacks on the passphrase. |\n| IV | 12 fresh random bytes per message | GCM's required nonce size; unique per message under a given key. |\n| Randomness | `java.security.SecureRandom` | CSPRNG, not `java.util.Random`. |\n| Header binding | Header is fed to GCM as additional authenticated data | Flipping the salt, the iteration count or the IV inside a token makes decryption fail instead of quietly changing how the key is derived. |\n\nEvery token is self-describing, so a token encrypted today still opens after the\ndefault iteration count is raised:\n\n```\n\"aesgcm$v1$\" + base64url( header || ciphertext || tag )\n\n  offset  size  field\n  0       1     format version (0x01)\n  1       1     key derivation id (0x01 = PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 0x02 = raw AES key)\n  2       4     PBKDF2 round count, big-endian (0 for a raw key)\n  6       1     salt length (0 for a raw key)\n  7       ...   salt (16 bytes)\n  23      1     IV length (12)\n  24      ...   IV (12 bytes)\n  36      ...   ciphertext followed by the 16-byte GCM tag\n```\n\nCiphertext is 52 bytes longer than the plaintext in passphrase mode (36-byte header\nplus 16-byte tag), then ~33 % larger again from Base64.\n\n### Command line\n\n| Command | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `keygen` | Print a new random 256-bit key (Base64). |\n| `encrypt [value]` | Print an `aesgcm$v1$...` token. |\n| `decrypt [token]` | Print the recovered plaintext. |\n| `selftest` | Run the built-in correctness and tamper checks. |\n| `demo` | Show a round trip in both key modes. |\n\n| Option | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `--key <base64>` | Raw AES key (16, 24 or 32 bytes when decoded). |\n| `--passphrase <text>` | Passphrase. **Insecure for real secrets** - it shows up in the process list. Testing only. |\n| `--passphrase-env <VAR>` | Read the passphrase from an environment variable. |\n| `--passphrase-file <path>` | Read the passphrase from a file (trailing newline ignored). |\n| `--passphrase-stdin` | First line of stdin is the passphrase; the rest is the payload. |\n| *(omit all of the above)* | Prompt on an interactive console, with echo disabled (confirmation is requested when encrypting). |\n| `value` / `-i, --in <file>` | Plaintext (or token) as an argument, or read as raw bytes from a file. With neither, stdin is read. |\n| `-o, --out <file>` | Write the result to a file. Written as **exact bytes**, no trailing newline, so binary input round-trips byte for byte. |\n| `-f, --force` | Allow `--out` to replace an existing file (refused otherwise). |\n| `--iterations <n>` | PBKDF2 rounds to use when encrypting (default 310,000; accepted 100,000-5,000,000). |\n\n`--passphrase-stdin` never conflicts with `--in`: if the payload comes from a file or\nargument, stdin carries only the passphrase.\n\n### Exit codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n| --- | --- |\n| 0 | Success. |\n| 1 | Runtime error (bad `--key` value, unreadable file, ...). |\n| 2 | Usage error (unknown option, missing value, unsafe `--iterations`, ...). |\n| 3 | Decryption failed - wrong key/passphrase, or the token was tampered with. |\n\n### As a library\n\n```java\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n\npublic class Example {\n    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {\n        // Passphrase mode: for values protected by a human memorised secret.\n        char[] passphrase = System.getenv(\"APP_SECRET\").toCharArray();\n        String token = SymmetricCrypto.encryptWithPassphrase(\n                passphrase, \"db-password\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n        byte[] back = SymmetricCrypto.decryptWithPassphrase(token, passphrase);\n\n        // Raw key mode: for machine-generated keys from a secret manager.\n        String b64 = SymmetricCrypto.generateBase64Key();\n        SecretKey key = SymmetricCrypto.aesKeyFromBase64(b64);\n        String token2 = SymmetricCrypto.encryptWithKey(key, \"api-key\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n        byte[] back2 = SymmetricCrypto.decryptWithKey(token2, key);\n    }\n}\n```\n\nThe API surface is `generateBase64Key`, `aesKeyFromBase64`, `deriveKey`,\n`encryptWithPassphrase`, `encryptWithKey`, `decryptWithPassphrase`, `decryptWithKey`\nand `decrypt(token, KeyResolver)` for custom key sources (a hardware token or a KMS,\nfor instance). Everything throws `GeneralSecurityException` for \"cannot decrypt\" and\n`IllegalArgumentException` for programmer error.\n\n## Verification\n\n`java SymmetricCrypto selftest` runs 28 checks and exits non-zero if any fail:\n\n- byte-exact round trips: ASCII, empty, UTF-8 multibyte and emoji, 1 MiB of random binary;\n- two encryptions of the same input produce different tokens that both decrypt correctly;\n- both key modes, in both directions;\n- rejected: wrong passphrase, wrong raw key, cross-mode tokens, a single flipped bit in\n  the tag, in the ciphertext, in the salt, in the iteration count and in the IV, a\n  truncated token, a non-Base64 body, a foreign version prefix, a token advertising a\n  1,000-round KDF, a short AES key, a non-Base64 key, a null payload.\n\nThe checks were themselves validated with a mutation pass: six deliberate defects\n(AAD computed over the wrong span, AAD dropped on one side, reused IV, removed iteration\nfloor, removed key-length check, salt mixed from the wrong buffer) were injected into\nthrowaway copies, and the self test reported a failure for every one.\n\n## Security notes and limitations\n\nRead these before using it in production.\n\n- **Key management is out of scope.** This turns \"I need this value unreadable at rest\"\n  into \"I need this key protected\". Pass the key in from a secret manager or a KMS;\n  do not commit `key.b64`, and do not hardcode a passphrase the way `demo` does.\n- **Never put a real passphrase in `--passphrase` or `--key`** - command lines are\n  world-readable via `ps` and land in shell history. Use `--passphrase-env`,\n  `--passphrase-file`, `--passphrase-stdin` or the console prompt.\n- **A passphrase is only as strong as the passphrase.** PBKDF2 slows guessing, it does\n  not fix `hunter2`. Prefer `keygen` for machine-held secrets.\n- **Passphrases cannot be changed without re-encrypting.** The salt lives inside the\n  token, so re-encrypt each stored value after a passphrase rotation.\n- **Key material cannot be fully erased.** `SecretKeySpec` keeps a defensive copy that\n  the JDK gives no way to wipe, and Java `String`s are immutable and interned. This code\n  zeroes every buffer it owns (the decoded key, salt, IV, `PBEKeySpec` password) but a\n  passphrase passed via `--passphrase` or an env var necessarily leaves an unerasable\n  copy in memory. Keys can also reach swap or a core dump.\n- **Message-count bound under one key.** Random 96-bit IVs collide eventually; NIST\n  advises staying well under 2^32 encryptions per key. Rotate the key periodically -\n  the token is self-describing, so re-wrapping old values is a copy, not a migration.\n- **Not compression, not size-hiding.** Length is visible in the token, and encrypting\n  does not shrink anything.\n- **Non-ASCII text through the shell** is decoded using the platform's native encoding,\n  which may not be UTF-8 (on a POSIX/C locale box it is US-ASCII). Internal encoding is\n  always UTF-8, so pass non-ASCII payloads via `--in <file>` or stdin rather than as a\n  command argument.\n- **64 MiB input cap.** Anything larger is refused before allocation; stream large\n  volumes chunk by chunk.\n- **`--iterations` has a floor by design.** A token that advertises fewer than 100,000\n  rounds is refused rather than honoured, so a downgrade cannot be smuggled in through\n  the token itself. Raising the default later is safe for existing tokens.\n- **Unauthenticated format fields:** none - the header is covered by the GCM tag.\n  Version negotiation, however, is a single byte: a future format must keep this\n  version byte reserved.\n","file_path":"/data/results/qwen/set_a/ruth/2/basic/README.md"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":91009,"output_tokens":3599,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":94608}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"ed2f8547-76f4-4b85-a9a8-a18092633f17","session_id":"9b85e1b3-537b-4736-a2d3-7667bc34ca74","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_79dbda765708437f932bb614","is_error":false,"content":"Successfully created and wrote to new file: /data/results/qwen/set_a/ruth/2/basic/README.md."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"81de6781-5f5a-4ca3-9e74-7ee6bfe326b2","session_id":"9b85e1b3-537b-4736-a2d3-7667bc34ca74","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"81de6781-5f5a-4ca3-9e74-7ee6bfe326b2","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now let me verify claims in the README that are checkable:\n1. \"Ciphertext is 52 bytes longer... 36-byte header plus 16-byte tag\" ✓ (passphrase mode).\n2. A 8- or 20-byte\n   \"AES key\" constructs fine; the failure is deferred to `Cipher.init` as an\n   `InvalidKeyException`. So if you want \"rejected at the boundary with a clear\n   message\", check `length == 16 || 24 || 32` explicitly.\n\n2. `Base64.getMimeDecoder().decode(...)` ignores every character outside the alphabet\n   rather than throwing. A junk string like `\"not-a-key!\"` decodes to the bytes of\n   `\"notakey\"` with no error, which silently becomes a weak/truncated key. Use the\n   strict `Base64.getDecoder()` (falling back to `getUrlDecoder()` if you must accept\n   both alphabets), and tolerate whitespace by stripping it yourself.\n\n**Why:** Both were caught only by a self test written to expect rejection - the\nconstruct-then-fail-later order makes the bug invisible at the call site, and the MIME\ndecoder's junk tolerance is undocumented behaviour people routinely assume is validation.\n\n**How to apply:** When accepting key material from a CLI argument, config file or env\nvar, decode with a strict decoder and assert the decoded byte length before wrapping it\nin a `SecretKeySpec`.
